What we are planning this year and what is happening from the federal side in the field of quality is a lot: We defined 2015 as a quality year. Forwarding still: The Article-15a agreement contains for the first time a quality target key that is financially subordinated. So this is the first time that a care key is fixed throughout the country – in the case of the 0 to 3 year olds from one to four and in the case of the larger ones from one to ten – which is implemented and promoted at the country level. So that's a very important milestone on this road. Secondly, we will hold a quality symposium in April, which will explain precisely these issues, including those countries which are concerned, among other things, with what the quality criteria are and how we can continue to work there. In May there is a symposium on men in kindergarten in Innsbruck. This is also an essential building block of quality in the kindergarten, also a support element in the article-15a agreement. That is to say, in the Article 15a agreement, we also have various incentives to raise quality, and the countries now have to collect and do this. We're there, I think, on a very good path.

[[10:02:43]] 1. Point Report by the Parliamentary Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection on ‘Walking at the End of Life’ (491 D.B.)
ParlaMint-AT_2015-03-26-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00066_d8e5236	This leads us to the 1st place. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. As First of all, Mrs Mag. Mag. Aubauer. – As Mrs Aubauer is not in the room, I give the floor to Mr Dr. Jarolim. Voluntary speaking time limit: 5 minutes. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-03-26-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00066_d8e5264	Madam President. Mrs. Bundesminis-terin! Ladies and gentlemen! Today we are talking about the Committee of Inquiry on “Walking at the End of Life”, which I see – and I hope, even from the point of view of everyone else! – has been dealt with in rare harmony and in a common effort. That is why I can certainly say this for Mr Aubauer and mention many of the points we have developed here through all the political groups. It's about dignity at the end of life. – That was the theme of the Enquete Commission, and we have made a total of eight events to explore a topic that is rather one of those issues that one does not like to deal with. We are living in a modern time when science is making continuous new discoveries and securing new developments. We are dealing with this, and we are always finding new solutions, but there are also points with which we are less concerned, where we look a little away, partly consciously and partly unconsciously. This also includes the situation and state of life at the end of life, of one's own life, but also the situation of friends, acquaintances and in total in society: We often think about it, but then we suppress it again, because we are usually unable to deal with the subject emotionally. That was one of the points that we have been working on in the Committee on Inquiry, namely to discuss how to support people at the end of their lives in different situations such as diseases and disease. We know that there is the possibility of consciously dealing with this situation in order to do the best possible to separate from life in the circle of his loved ones either through pain therapy or through a corresponding death accompaniment. With regard to hospice and palliative care – another name for pain therapy and death care – we have highlighted the various aspects, and a large number of experts have informed us of their experiences. We want to build a model across Austria, ensuring that in the future there will be no more people suffering from pain or living in loneliness, who no one cares for and who is in a hospital. We are concerned with the fact that we are Consciously strive to support those affected in their particular situation in such a way as to create the best possible framework conditions. I am very pleased that we have found extraordinary human and material access in this regard and that, with a few exceptions, all at the end of our events have expressed the same opinion as you can provide the best possible support. Self-determination is a very essential point in this regard, and we have therefore also decided to improve the possibility of determining, in the form of patient provision, how to deal with one's own fate, when one comes into situations that can also become fatal. It is very important that you also create opportunities for doctors to deal with this self-determination. In the future, there will also be wider information and knowledge about such decisions. We want to make sure that the largest possible number of people themselves have what happens when they come into medical treatment. People themselves should determine what happens when they become unconscious or under what conditions they no longer want to be treated. There must also be the right to decide for yourself that you do not want to have certain conditions in which you depend more or less on machines. This must also be enforced so far that doctors do not ultimately let the machines run out of uncertainty, but that in such difficult situations the self-elected form of dignity is possible. We have also learned a lot through philosophical treatises. The representative of the deacony, Mr. Michael Chalupka, spoke of the fact that there is a right, but no duty to life and that every person must ultimately decide for himself when he divorces from his life. In this context, of course, there was also a discussion about the situation if someone wants to get out of life for reasons of despair, because he has pain that cannot be treated medically. We all probably know this from our own circle of relatives and acquaintances. The question is: Should there be support? In the end, we have not resolved this question, but we will certainly continue to discuss it, in the way that it is not a party-political discussion, but that we are aware of our role and responsibility towards the population in this respect, in order to do the best possible in this sense. - Thank you.

Point Report of the Scientific Committee on the report of the Federal Minister for Science, Research and Economy on the projects of the European Union 2015 (III-146/512 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2015-03-26-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00066_d8e7053	We're now on the 2nd. Agenda item. First of all, Mr. Dr. Kassinger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-03-26-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00066_d8e7077	Mr President. Mr. Vice-Chancellor! High house! We are discussing today in plenary the report of the Federal Minister for Science, Research and Economy on the European Union's projects in the fields of economics and science, i.e. the projects of the European Commission and the European Council for 2015, which are within the scope of this ministry. I have chosen two points from the field of economic activity, which I would like to address specifically, namely the “strengthened industrial base” and the “energy” area. We live in a globalized economy. This is a fact, whether we like it or not, and the globalized economy is characterised, among other things, by the fact that capital is looking for the place of the best interest rate and the industry is looking for the place where the best framework conditions prevail. It is therefore up to European policy to make Europe's location as attractive as possible to industry. Of course, it is also in the longer term the task of Austrian policy to make Austria's location as attractive as possible within Europe. Why is that so important? – We believe that a strong industrial base is the basic prerequisite for economic growth, employment and innovation. Much is involved in industry. In industry, small and medium-sized enterprises depend on the service providers, but also on research and development, especially application-oriented research. This means that it is the policy's task to create optimal location factors. What are the location factors that present problems or give rise to the need for action that are the challenges to politics – not taxative, without a claim to completeness? First of all, the quality and training of the people. We have a permanent construction site in Austria, especially in the field of education. The second location factor of importance is the so-called input cost, because the bill still works in economic terms: Income minus cost is the same result. And if the proceeds are higher than the costs, the result is positive. This is ultimately the objective of economic action. What is it like with the input costs in Europe and Austria? – You know, we have enormously high personnel costs, enormously high additional wage costs, which are mainly due to high taxes on the labour factor, but also due to high social charges, i.e. no optimal framework conditions. We have high raw material costs. We also need to consider the extent to which a weakening of the euro is not a huge disadvantage, because we in the world also buy raw materials and these raw materials become more expensive by 25.30 percent. So it is not all good in the sense of promoting the export economy if the euro is so weak. These effects need to be looked more closely. Unfortunately, we also have very high energy costs in Europe. One of the reasons is that energy costs are very high. There is a high level of taxation and taxation on staff, as has been said, but also a high level of taxation on business results. The government has now increased the capital income tax, and it is said that only shareholders and dividend owners – in reality every small GesmbH with 2.5 percent Kest increase – do not improve the framework conditions. We have environmental and climate protection requirements. It should be noted here that a policy has to be made with measure and objective. The long-term planning and predictability of economic policy is also very important. Next location factor: bureaucratic barriers, EU bureaucracy sample. In addition, SMEs are likely to be harder than industry. Central is also a corresponding market for local customers, the so-called single market. There are also developments, problematic developments, that purchasing power is falling. Another location factor is a functioning rule of law, a state legal system, the rule of law. We have thank God no mafia parallel structures in Austria, but the corporate special rights in the TTIP, which allows companies to sue states in private arbitration courts, are incompatible with a developed rule of law, in our opinion. In short: Europe is in the global competition of its locations. I have the impression that Europe needs to be very careful here to keep its position. The report defines an entire set of objectives and measures. The European Commission and the Council want to strengthen the competitiveness of European industry, e.g. through infrastructure development, by promoting entrepreneurship, through innovation and skills, by harmonising the costs of energy and raw materials in international conditions and ensuring the availability of these inputs, by increasing the share of industry in EU GDP to 20 percent by 2020. These are all good goals. They sound good, we support them. But the devil is always in the detail of implementation, and we are eager to see how the implementation will succeed. The Austrian position on this is, among other things: The regulatory framework for businesses will be improved and structural reforms addressed. I can only say: How true, how true! I hear the message well, I alone lack faith. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] At the same time, however, I must say that we expressly welcome and support two concrete measures taken by the Federal Government in this regard. On the one hand, this is the development of an Austrian site strategy for more than 300 leading companies together with 40 CEOs. On the other hand, this is the “Industrie 4.0” project to support structural change towards a networked industry and intelligent manufacturing processes. The energy sector is also central to the positive development of the European economic location. In the energy sector, the EU has an import dependency of 53 percent, which according to energy sources is as follows: Crude oil 88 percent, natural gas 66 percent, solid fuels 42 percent, uranium 95 percent. We support the following energy policy objectives: Reducing import dependence and import quotas, in particular with regard to fossil fuels. Reduction of the share of fossil fuels or increase in the share of renewable energy. Full exit from nuclear energy. These are very big research topics. Similarly, the topic of forwarding energy from A to B. The resources should be bundled and increased. We liberals believe that today's most burning problem is the problem of exploding unemployment. What is the point of this proposal? – It is about three main points. First of all, it is about ensuring the equality of foreign students within the meaning of EU standards. Secondly, it is about avoiding double funding in the future, and thirdly, it is about clarifying that funding and aid to students are only carried out in cases of social need. What sounds good at the first moment, however, has a number of problems in itself from a liberal point of view: for example, this recently mentioned eligibility of funding. We of the FPÖ believe that not only should the social factor count, i.e. social eligibility, but there must also be a principle of performance. In particular, we want to avoid further immigration into our social system under the title of study. I think it is really necessary to sharpen it accordingly. With regard to the double promotions that have previously been addressed by me: It is clear that there should not be these, and the FPÖ naturally supports the abolition of overlaps, but in this concrete draft this is only aimed at the Federal Republic of Germany. We believe that it is also necessary to inculcate the other countries within the EU accordingly. It would be important to enter into negotiations with EU countries. Maybe just a few figures in advance: The budget includes €3.06 billion. If one calculates this by the numbers of students studying here in Austria, one concludes that about one billion of the budget is spent on students from abroad. In addition, there has been an increasing trend in recent years. I can only point to this: In 2010/2011 40 percent of students at the university in Austria came from abroad, and in 2013/2014 it was already 44 percent. In the course of negotiations, we could ensure that we obtain only half of the money we spend on students from EU countries – that is about €600 million –: 300 million would be €300 million for our universities, which we could invest in the further expansion of quality. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, from our point of view, it is also time to really consider entering into concrete negotiations with the other EU countries. There are already positive examples in Europe where such negotiations have been very, very well conducted. For example, I can refer to the countries of the Nordic Council. There is already an agreement between Denmark, Finland, Iceland, Norway and Sweden, which makes it quite easy to calculate, pay, and thus balance some imbalances for the students in the respective countries. I would suggest that this is the case for Austria and Germany. This would in itself be a good pilot project, first and foremost with Germany – there are the Most of the students concerned – to negotiate and evaluate: How many Austrian students do we have in Germany and how many vice versa? Perhaps you can really think about how to compensate for it in a sustainable way. [[Complementary of the Abg. Barbara Rosenkranz.]] Should this result in a similar pattern, a contract that is really feasible, these negotiations should be applied to the rest of the EU. There are already examples in the field of study, for example in the medical community, where European solutions were very likely to be found. I can remind you: In negotiations with other EU countries, Austria has ensured that there is a certain quota for medical training places in Austria. In this context, I should also like to point out that this agreement was already signed on 31 December. It will expire on 1 December 2016. I believe that this would be a good time not only to renegotiate the matter with the doctors but really to enter into negotiations on the balance between the EU countries. The FPÖ is also in very good company with these demands. For example: Examinations will not be accepted, examinations will be recognized, forced study plan changes, there is no place in a compulsory practice or at all no study place, and, and, and. – There are countless such obstacles, however, all in common is that students are always in a weaker position towards universities and professors. Unfortunately, the students often have no opportunity to oppose it. [[President Hofer shall take over the chairmanship.]] That's why the student ombudsmanship is so important. It is a contact point for students, a place that accepts these problems and tries to solve them by talking to the rectorate, with the institutes, with professors, with other students or also with politics. The problem so far was only that the ombudsman had partly too few legal competences to be effective in some cases. This is exactly what this proposal for the Higher Quality Assurance Act is about to amend. This request is, as has been said, a common request from all parties. Unfortunately – and I must say that too – this is an exception and far too rare, but a positive example of how it can be possible to make a little bit more constructively even through discussions in the Committee on a cross-party basis. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Unfortunately, the common practice, which has often been criticized, is that the opposition's applications are almost always postponed and thus permanently shaken. Unfortunately, hundreds of opposition applications do not come to the plenary each year. By the way, this is also a process – and I must say this to the colleagues of SPÖ and ÖVP – which is not all right and which should be urgently covered by you! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] If you do not agree to an application in a committee, then simply reject it – and give the opportunity to discuss it here and explain why it has been rejected, especially when it comes to requests from committees that have rarely had anything in the plenary session. Give the opportunity to discuss this too! However, as has been said, I am very pleased that my ideas were taken up by the original request – I already made a request in August 2014 on the subject, the ombudsman’s office – and have now become a common request. I am very concerned that the ombudsman's competences will be expanded and that the ombudsman's office will be given the opportunity to help students even better. What exactly is it about? . This proposal entails exactly two important changes. First: The ombudsman can also work on his own perception in the future. So far, she could only become active if a student has gone to the Ombudsman with a concrete abuse and has asked him to do so. The problem, however, was that many students did not. Why? – Because on the one hand they may not have known that there is the ombudsman. Perhaps they had some inhibitions, or maybe they thought it didn't actually bring much and that there was no solution. We would already be here at the second important change, namely in the matter of confidentiality. Of course, it is important that the Ombudsman is obliged to remain silent, especially when it comes to personal data from students, and of course especially when the student wishes it. The problem so far was only that the obligation to keep secret was far too strict. This has resulted in the ombudsman's not even being able to report or make known in any way, at which universities there are which misunderstandings and how this specific university has responded when they have been addressed. We want to achieve that the ombudsman can do exactly that, which is exactly what he can make known. With this application, such universities are no longer covered by the obligation of secrecy and can be made publicly known. This gives the ombudsman some of the necessary effectiveness to combat unge- Do justice, because no university in the world would somehow like to know what is going wrong, or in the end have a bad reputation. Another important aspect, especially for Parliament and for our Members, is that we also have more, more comprehensive and better information available, so that in the future we too will be able to make better decisions for our students and also for universities. Thank you.

For example, the construction of textile factories is so low that these textile factories, together with the working people, such as at the Rana Plaza in Bangladesh, can be destroyed and hundreds of deaths can be complained. This too is more than unfair competition and more than unfair because cheap goods are produced at the expense of human life. We know that millions of people in the world, including a few thousand in Austria, are working under slave-like conditions, that there are still children's exploitable work both in products and in services. We know, for example, that in some companies in China, labour pressure is so high that people commit suicide there because they cannot cope with this pressure. We know that there are very, very many countries where ILO core labour standards, such as guarantees of a fair wage, the possibility of organizing themselves trade unions or other basic labour law matters, are not respected. This will also result in unfair competition because it is impossible to compare the working conditions, the costs for the production of products or services with those in Austria. I think we should do something about that. We have many possibilities to do so, not just on directives and laws relating to unfair competition. The Federal Procurement Act, which is being modernised under an EU directive, offers a lot of opportunities to do so. There would be a chance in the Corporate Code, for example, to look at company due diligence obligations to ensure that, even in sub-sub-sub-sub-contracts, there is no circumvention of labour rights. It would be possible to work with a lot of approaches to ensure decent working conditions around the world. I would like to stress: In the 1960s, we joined the Human Rights Pact on Civil and Political Rights and that on Economic, Cultural and Social Rights. In its final report at the end of 2013, the WSK Committee, which reviews the latter, recommends that we take more legal measures to enable companies with headquarters in Austria to be held liable here, too, by Austrian companies that engage in business activities abroad and commit human rights violations. We must be able to take this into account here too. I really think it's important to break your head over it. There's a lot to do. I would be pleased if we were to follow this call from the United Nations and the WSC Committee very soon and take legal action in this direction very soon. – Thank you very much.

Again and again, there are incidents in which police officers are killed or seriously injured. I would like to mention in particular that, on the one hand, police officers are given measures – compulsory powers – to give them the necessary armor, and on the other hand, that, in the event of identified errors, for example in the event of accusations, the visit of courses or others, no definition is possible, which is particularly important for officials, for example, to get the indebtedness, the loss of the office is also associated with this; we have even reduced this from one year to six months. Even pension claims can be recognised or held back in the event of seizures. This is not defined in every professional group as it is with the police. These measures can be taken in the same way as a false witness statement.
